People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most days there’s kind of this heavy, flat feeling that follows me around. Like even when I’m just pushing my cart down an empty hallway at work, or sitting with my family trying to act normal, it’s still there. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a while. I’d say about three years, give or take. It kind of started after our contractor switched us to rotating evening and overnight shifts at the bigger properties. I kept thinking I’d adjust and it wasn’t that big a deal, but it just sort of stayed. Does that sound like the kind of timeframe you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: Um… not really anxious, I don’t think. I mean, I get a little tense about normal stuff, like work or whether I’m keeping up, but I wouldn’t say I’m on edge all the time. At least, I haven’t really noticed that as the main thing. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like, such as hobbies or spending time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I still do those things sometimes, like being around family, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m enjoying them the same way. Maybe I’m just tired or distracted. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic. I guess it’s kind of hard for me to tell what counts as “not enjoying” versus just… being in a rut, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasurable but no longer enjoy?\n\nPatient: I guess… not clearly, no. Like, I can’t point to one specific thing and say, “I used to love that and now I feel nothing.” It’s more that things feel kind of muted sometimes, but I still do them here and there. I’m not sure if that counts for what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. I mean, I haven’t really been tracking it closely or anything, but my clothes seem to fit about the same. Nothing that’s stood out to me, at least. Is that okay to say if I’m not totally sure?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep’s been kind of off, I guess. I work overnights, so I know that can mess with things, and I don’t want to make too much of it, but sometimes after a shift I’ll be really tired and still just lie there feeling wired. And even when I do get what seems like enough hours, I wake up feeling like I didn’t really rest. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there activities that you used to find pleasurable but now feel neutral or unenjoyable about?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a clear way. I mean, some things maybe don’t feel as exciting as they probably should, but I wouldn’t say I’ve totally stopped enjoying them. It’s more like I have to be in the right mood, I guess. Sorry, I don’t know if that’s a helpful answer.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.   },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

Is that the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. I still do things I used to like, and sometimes they’re okay… maybe not as easy to get into as before, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I guess it depends on the day. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do, more than I probably should. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I catch myself thinking other guys my age would’ve adjusted by now or moved up, you know? And I’m still just trying not to miss small things at work. So then I start wondering if maybe I’m just not really cut out for more than this. Is that what you mean by blaming myself?\n\nDoctor: Are there things that used to give you pleasure but now feel like a chore or don't give you pleasure at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say they give me no pleasure at all. Some things just feel… harder to start, maybe. Like I can still enjoy something once I’m doing it, but getting myself to do it can feel like more effort than it should. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, though. Is that kind of what you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most days there’s kind of this heavy, flat feeling that follows me around. Like even when I’m just pushing my cart down an empty hallway at work, or sitting with my family trying to act normal, it’s still there. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a while. I’d say about three years, give or take. It kind of started after our contractor switched us to rotating evening and overnight shifts at the bigger properties. I kept thinking I’d adjust and it wasn’t that big a deal, but it just sort of stayed. Does that sound like the kind of timeframe you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: Um… not really anxious, I don’t think. I mean, I get a little tense about normal stuff, like work or whether I’m keeping up, but I wouldn’t say I’m on edge all the time. At least, I haven’t really noticed that as the main thing. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like, such as hobbies or spending time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I still do those things sometimes, like being around family, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m enjoying them the same way. Maybe I’m just tired or distracted. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic. I guess it’s kind of hard for me to tell what counts as “not enjoying” versus just… being in a rut, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasurable but no longer enjoy?\n\nPatient: I guess… not clearly, no. Like, I can’t point to one specific thing and say, “I used to love that and now I feel nothing.” It’s more that things feel kind of muted sometimes, but I still do them here and there. I’m not sure if that counts for what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. I mean, I haven’t really been tracking it closely or anything, but my clothes seem to fit about the same. Nothing that’s stood out to me, at least. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a while. I’d say about three years, give or take. It kind of started after our contractor switched us to rotating evening and overnight shifts at the bigger properties. I kept thinking I’d adjust and it wasn’t that big a deal, but it just sort of stayed. Does that sound like the kind of timeframe you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: Um… not really anxious, I don’t think. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like, such as hobbies or spending time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I still do those things sometimes, like being around family, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m enjoying them the same way. I guess it’s kind of hard for me to tell what counts as “not enjoying” versus just… being in a rut, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasurable but no longer enjoy?\n\nPatient: I guess… not clearly, no. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi… thanks for seeing me. I guess I’m here because my focus has been kind of off. Not terrible, I mean, I’m still getting things done, but my head just doesn’t seem to stay on task like it used to.\n\nLike at work, I’ll be doing inventory or go to the supply closet and then forget what I went in there for. And I’ve been trying to study for a supervisor certification, but I keep reading the same line over and over and not really taking it in. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing I should come in for, but it’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most days there’s kind of this heavy, flat feeling that follows me around. Like even when I’m just pushing my cart down an empty hallway at work, or sitting with my family trying to act normal, it’s still there. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a while. I’d say about three years, give or take. It kind of started after our contractor switched us to rotating evening and overnight shifts at the bigger properties. I kept thinking I’d adjust and it wasn’t that big a deal, but it just sort of stayed. Does that sound like the kind of timeframe you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: Um… not really anxious, I don’t think. I mean, I get a little tense about normal stuff, like work or whether I’m keeping up, but I wouldn’t say I’m on edge all the time. At least, I haven’t really noticed that as the main thing. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like, such as hobbies or spending time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I still do those things sometimes, like being around family, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m enjoying them the same way. Maybe I’m just tired or distracted. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic. I guess it’s kind of hard for me to tell what counts as “not enjoying” versus just… being in a rut, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasurable but no longer enjoy?\n\nPatient: I guess… not clearly, no. Like, I can’t point to one specific thing and say, “I used to love that and now I feel nothing.” It’s more that things feel kind of muted sometimes, but I still do them here and there. I’m not sure if that counts for what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. I mean, I haven’t really been tracking it closely or anything, but my clothes seem to fit about the same. Nothing that’s stood out to me, at least. Is that okay to say if I’m not totally sure?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep’s been kind of off, I guess. I work overnights, so I know that can mess with things, and I don’t want to make too much of it, but sometimes after a shift I’ll be really tired and still just lie there feeling wired. And even when I do get what seems like enough hours, I wake up feeling like I didn’t really rest. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there activities that you used to find pleasurable but now feel neutral or unenjoyable about?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a clear way. I mean, some things maybe don’t feel as exciting as they probably should, but I wouldn’t say I’ve totally stopped enjoying them. It’s more like I have to be in the right mood, I guess. Sorry, I don’t know if that’s a helpful answer.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, not anything significant that I’ve noticed. I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I guess I can’t say 100%, but my clothes fit about the same and nobody’s mentioned anything. Is that the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. I still do things I used to like, and sometimes they’re okay… maybe not as easy to get into as before, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I guess it depends on the day. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do, more than I probably should. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I catch myself thinking other guys my age would’ve adjusted by now or moved up, you know? And I’m still just trying not to miss small things at work. So then I start wondering if maybe I’m just not really cut out for more than this. Is that what you mean by blaming myself?\n\nDoctor: Are there things that used to give you pleasure but now feel like a chore or don't give you pleasure at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say they give me no pleasure at all. Some things just feel… harder to start, maybe. Like I can still enjoy something once I’m doing it, but getting myself to do it can feel like more effort than it should. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, though. Is that kind of what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you had anyone comment on your weight loss recently?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, nobody’s said anything to me about losing weight. My clothes seem about the same, I think. I haven’t been tracking it closely, though—should I be?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I haven’t noticed my weight changing, at least not in any obvious way. I don’t really weigh myself much, but my clothes fit about the same, so… I’d say probably not.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any significant changes in your appetite recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s been anything significant, no. I mean, some days I’m not that hungry, but it doesn’t seem like a big change or anything. I still eat pretty normally, I think. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like, such as hobbies or spending time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I still do those things sometimes, like being around family, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m enjoying them the same way. Maybe I’m just tired or distracted. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic. I guess it’s kind of hard for me to tell what counts as “not enjoying” versus just… being in a rut, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasurable but no longer enjoy?\n\nPatient: I guess… not clearly, no. Like, I can’t point to one specific thing and say, “I used to love that and now I feel nothing.” It’s more that things feel kind of muted sometimes, but I still do them here and there. I’m not sure if that counts for what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. I mean, I haven’t really been tracking it closely or anything, but my clothes seem to fit about the same. Nothing that’s stood out to me, at least. Is that okay to say if I’m not totally sure?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep’s been kind of off, I guess. I work overnights, so I know that can mess with things, and I don’t want to make too much of it, but sometimes after a shift I’ll be really tired and still just lie there feeling wired. And even when I do get what seems like enough hours, I wake up feeling like I didn’t really rest. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there activities that you used to find pleasurable but now feel neutral or unenjoyable about?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a clear way. I mean, some things maybe don’t feel as exciting as they probably should, but I wouldn’t say I’ve totally stopped enjoying them. It’s more like I have to be in the right mood, I guess. Sorry, I don’t know if that’s a helpful answer.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, not anything significant that I’ve noticed. I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I guess I can’t say 100%, but my clothes fit about the same and nobody’s mentioned anything. Is that the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. I still do things I used to like, and sometimes they’re okay… maybe not as easy to get into as before, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I guess it depends on the day. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do, more than I probably should. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I catch myself thinking other guys my age would’ve adjusted by now or moved up, you know? And I’m still just trying not to miss small things at work. So then I start wondering if maybe I’m just not really cut out for more than this. Is that what you mean by blaming myself?\n\nDoctor: Are there things that used to give you pleasure but now feel like a chore or don't give you pleasure at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say they give me no pleasure at all. Some things just feel… harder to start, maybe. Like I can still enjoy something once I’m doing it, but getting myself to do it can feel like more effort than it should. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, though. Is that kind of what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you had anyone comment on your weight loss recently?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, nobody’s said anything to me about losing weight. My clothes seem about the same, I think. I haven’t been tracking it closely, though—should I be?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I haven’t noticed my weight changing, at least not in any obvious way. I don’t really weigh myself much, but my clothes fit about the same, so… I’d say probably not.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any significant changes in your appetite recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s been anything significant, no. I mean, some days I’m not that hungry, but it doesn’t seem like a big change or anything. I still eat pretty normally, I think. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing, but now you don't enjoy them at all?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I guess nothing comes to mind that I don’t enjoy at all now. I still do some of the same things, maybe not always with a ton of enthusiasm, but I wouldn’t say I’ve completely stopped enjoying them. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing that you no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really, I don’t think. There are things I maybe don’t get as excited about as I used to, but I wouldn’t say I get no pleasure from them anymore. I still can enjoy things sometimes. I hope that answers what you’re asking.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
